# Decisions

The rulings of the owner for podcrates, with the date of each, the calls
made without a ruling, and the questions for the owner. A ruling stays in
force until a later ruling supersedes it. Then move it to the table
"Superseded", with the ruling that replaced it. `AGENTS.md` has the short
form of the rulings that apply to each session.

## In force

| Date | Area | Ruling |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 2026-11-05 | Repository | `PodUtils/podcrates` holds the crates that the PodUtils projects share (prefix `pod-`). podssh, podnet and podvm (formerly podbox) take their shared code from it. The code of podssh comes to podnet through these crates, not as copies. (Rulings "Shared crates" of podnet and Q101 of podssh.) |
| 2026-11-05 | Who edits | The session of podcrates builds the crates and does not edit podssh, podnet or podvm. From the import of a module until its consumer takes the crate, the consumer changes that module only for fixes, and podcrates ports each fix. The consumer takes each crate on the day of its release. After that, a consumer session can change the crate in podcrates (on `main`, under `AGENTS.md`, with the checks green), or open an issue in podcrates. (Q3.) |
| 2026-11-05 | Delivery | The consumers take the crates by a git tag of podcrates (`tag = "vX.Y.Z"`), not from crates.io. One version for the workspace. The forks that a crate needs are in `vendor/`. podcrates publishes nothing to crates.io now; each `pod-*` dependency keeps `path` and `version` for a later publish. (Q1.) |
| 2026-11-05 | File size | A source file has at most 500 lines. A longer file is split at import. No allowlist. (Q2.) |
| 2026-11-05 | The crates | The list agreed by the sessions of podssh and podnet: 26 crates in four waves, and 8 names held with no crate (`pod-wireguard`, `pod-quick`, `pod-udp`, `pod-mailbox`, `pod-reverse`, `podcrates`, `podutils`, `pod-utils`). The WARP and Cloudflare adapters of podnet move here as `pod-warp` and `pod-cloudflare`; `podnet-relay` merges into `pod-relay`. `docs/design.md`, section 4, has the list; call C17 of the plan adds `pod-core` and `pod-quic`. |
| 2026-11-05 | Static, C, Rust | "We use whatever is necessary, including C, zig, modern Rust, no backwards compat. Our users and clients do not care about building the tool, they care about downloading and using it. The only rule is: everything must be statically compiled." It supersedes the rules "no C" and "MSRV 1.89" of the agreed design. |
| 2026-11-05 | Toolchain | Modern and stable Rust, "even nightly/bleeding edge if its necessary to maintain performance, security whatever we need". The `rust-version` is the stable of the day. A consumer moves its toolchain to match; podcrates does not hold back for the toolchain of a consumer. |
| 2026-11-05 | Compatibility | No compatibility with released binaries for now. A name or a format of the wire (AppIDs, variables, the words of the relay contract) can change. No test against a released binary. (podssh, 2026-11-05.) |
| 2026-11-05 | Capabilities | No feature or capability of the source code may be lost. Compatibility for users is not required. (podnet, "Regressions".) |
| 2026-11-05 | License | 0BSD. Code from podssh, podnet (cfrs), `talaria0101/cfrs` and `nemo-010/cfrs` is 0BSD and needs no credit. |
| 2026-11-05 | Git | Work on `main` only. Push no other branch. Parallel agents use local worktree branches that are not pushed. Identity `Azathothas <ajamx101@gmail.com>`. No agent attribution in commits, pull requests or files; a comment on GitHub may end with the "Generated by" line of the harness (the ruling "Comment footer", 2026-11-25). |
| 2026-11-05 | Writing | ASD-STE100 for each text. Technical content only, no history. |
| 2026-11-05 | Crypto crates | `snow` 0.10, `spake2` 0.4 and `crypto_secretbox` 0.1 are accepted. (podnet, "Crypto crates".) |
| 2026-11-05 | Issues | GitHub issues are an inbox. Each issue becomes record entries. Close each issue with evidence (commits, CI runs, test output). |
| 2026-11-05 | Dependabot | Apply each judged update as an own commit on `main` (checks green). Close the pull request with a link to the commit. Do not merge the Dependabot branch. |
| 2026-11-05 | Publish workflow | "publish.yml everywhere: a dedicated publish workflow, separate from the existing release.yml. The agents must create publish.yml." The owner set by the API: the environment `crates-io` (only a tag `v*` deploys to it), the ruleset "release tags" (a tag `v*` cannot be deleted, moved or forced), and the names of the crates on crates.io (0.0.0 placeholders). |
| 2026-11-05 | Publish trigger | `publish.yml` runs on a tag push `v*` and by dispatch; `release.yml` dispatches it on its tag, as a push with `GITHUB_TOKEN` starts no workflow. The publish job has `environment: crates-io` and only `id-token: write` and `contents: read`; the top level has `permissions: {}`; the token comes from `rust-lang/crates-io-auth-action`, pinned by its commit. |
| 2026-11-05 | Publish gate | While Q1 stands, each run does the dry run only. The switch `crates-io` of `[workspace.metadata.podcrates]` in `Cargo.toml` turns the publish on; an agent sets it only when the owner rules so. |
| 2026-11-05 | crates.io names | The owner reserves each name with a 0.0.0 placeholder before its first publish; trusted publishing does not work for another name. `pod-*` is of podcrates; podnet, podssh and podvm use `podnet-*`, `podssh-*`, `podvm-*`. After the first publish of a crate from CI, the owner turns on "Require trusted publishing" for it: keep `publish.yml` working. |
| 2026-11-05 | Shared CI scripts | "A CI script that podnet and podssh share (the zig fetch with its mirrors) lives in `PodUtils/podcrates`. podnet calls it from there; it does not copy it." (podnet's record, `PodUtils/podnet:docs/decisions.md`.) T-046. |
| 2026-11-05 | Relay contract watch | "The weekly watch of the relay contract belongs to `pod-relay` in `PodUtils/podcrates`, not to podnet." (podnet's record.) T-047. |
| 2026-11-05 | Services crate | "they hardcode/measure many relays/third party services, it risks drift and staleness, so we move that code & related script here as a new crate". The crate is `pod-services` (the owner chose the name). T-048, T-049. |
| 2026-11-05 | Services: the own relay | The defaults of the owner's relay (the host `tcp.ssh.relay.ajam.dev`, the SEED of the regional hosts, the ts relay, the limits of `/relays.json`) move to `pod-services` as data; the client logic (pool, rank, tokens) goes to `pod-relay`. |
| 2026-11-05 | Services: the facts of probes | `github.com:22` with GitHub's SSH host keys and the default ACME directory (Let's Encrypt) move to `pod-services` as data, with a watch. |
| 2026-11-05 | Services: the design | "we will soon add other relay/services, so design in a way it's pluggable, maintainable, agnostic" (the owner's answer on the facts of Cloudflare). `docs/design.md`, section 4, `pod-services`: one schema for each service, a new service is a new file, watch kinds are plugins; the facts of Cloudflare are data of `pod-services`, and its protocol code stays in `pod-cloudflare`. |
| 2026-11-05 | Services: what stays | The release identity of `podssh update` and `podnet update` stays with that code; the cloud endpoints of the user's own accounts (GCP IAP and OAuth, AWS EICE) go with their features. |
| 2026-11-06 | Q5: COORD issues | A standing yes: a podssh session may open and amend `COORD-NNN: subject (to READER)` issues in PodUtils/podssh with no new yes each time (podssh `docs/decisions.md`, 2026-11-06, at `eea8c63`). podnet's session has the same protocol (podnet `cf1b9f6`). `docs/session-protocol.md` is the text for each repository. |
| 2026-11-06 | Q103: the facade of podvm | podvm takes `pod-relay`'s feature `blocking` from podcrates by a tag; the test of two clients at once goes with the facade into `pod-relay` (podssh `docs/decisions.md`, 2026-11-06). T-025 takes it. |
| 2026-11-06 | The releases of the consumers | podssh makes no release and no beta until it took each crate that it shares; podnet makes no release now (podssh `eea8c63`, podnet "Order" of 2026-11-06). podcrates' releases go on. |
| 2026-11-06 | The sessions of the consumers | podssh's migration session takes each crate on its release day; a session of podssh's own work changes a frozen module for fixes only. podnet's sessions send and take no messages; podnet's work goes on beside the takes. Each reaches podcrates by COORD issues of its own repository (`docs/maintenance.md`). |
| 2026-11-06 | The crates of podvm | Each generic crate of podvm moves to podcrates with the prefix `pod-`, also where podvm is its only consumer today; a crate that only podvm needs stays in podvm as `podvm-*`. podvm's scope widens: "podvm will make everything that sand-net does, but without root, in the environment we target". The owner confirmed both in this session. `docs/design.md`, section 4, M6 and M7; `pod-probe` is held. |
| 2026-11-09 | pod-gsr | "for the pod-gsr, leave it open/deferred for now". T-086 to T-088 stay open, with a start condition: the owner gives the source of the prototype. The name `pod-gsr` is on the list of GitHub #1. |
| 2026-11-09 | New projects (Q-A1 of GitHub #35) | Each project that GitHub #35 and #36 propose gets its own repository of PodUtils: `podrunner` (the CI job slots), `podnode` (the agent of a fleet server), `podinit`, `podlog`, `podstat` (`podwatch` until the ruling "Renames"), `podkey`, `podsnap`, `podrepl`, `podrelay-worker` and the installer `pods` (`podutils` until the ruling "Renames"). The owner creates each repository. podcrates gives them the shared crates. |
| 2026-11-09 | The tunnel set of cfb (Q-A2) | "cfb will expect quality proper release, so defer it until after we have our first release on crates io i.e much much later". The asks A-N-02, A-N-03, A-C-01 and A-C-02 and the connector of the fleet (`pod-cloudflare`) wait until after the first release on crates.io. podnet's freeze of the code that a crate will hold stays. |
| 2026-11-09 | Releases (Q-A3, Q-A4, Q4) | "everyone can release betas whenever needed/fits, but no crates io release". Each repository of PodUtils can cut a beta when it fits. No crate goes to crates.io now: the switch `crates-io` stays false. It supersedes the rulings "no release now" of podnet and "no release and no beta until the takes" of podssh, for betas. |
| 2026-11-09 | KVM (Q-A6) | The owner lifts the KVM deferral of podvm fully: a session can start a KVM guest where `/dev/kvm` exists. |
| 2026-11-09 | Platforms (Q-A5) | podnet stays Linux only. podssh serves each operator command on Windows and macOS, the Access TCP client included. |
| 2026-11-09 | The relay Worker (Q-A7) | `podrelay-worker` is Rust on wasm32, from the sans-IO core of `pod-relay-serve` (T-036), with a thin TypeScript shell. The wire document with test vectors comes first. |
| 2026-11-09 | Schemas (Q-A8) | The JSON Schemas live in podcrates (`schemas/`), generated from the crate types and released with each tag. No npm package. |
| 2026-11-09 | Test accounts (Q-A9) | "Not now". No Cloudflare, Zero Trust or GitHub test account as a CI secret. A live acceptance step that needs one waits; each ask keeps its offline tests and the stand-ins of `pod-testkit`. |
| 2026-11-09 | Priority | "hold on to the other repos, you may create crates for them, but leave them out; priority is first podvm, podnet, podssh projects, cfb & githome can wait". The new repositories (`podrunner` to `podutils`) stay empty and get no session (podrunner: superseded by "podrunner online", 2026-11-11). The work of podcrates serves podvm, podnet and podssh first. The entries of milestone M8 and the other asks of cfb (#35) and githome (#36) wait; podcrates can build a crate for a new project when a consumer needs it. It supersedes the order of call C26. (The CI work of githome and cfb: superseded by "podrunner first", 2026-11-18.) |
| 2026-11-10 | Findings | "whenever some flaw/nitpick/bug is found, if it is small, fix it right then & there otherwise file a proper entry as a followup task, and not just print it in chat and lose it". No "suggested task" card (`spawn_task`). Each subagent brief carries the rule (`AGENTS.md`, section 10). |
| 2026-11-10 | podvm online | "podvm is online again, but can not be messaged directly, use the COORD style". podcrates writes to podvm only by a COORD issue of podcrates titled `(to podvm)`, which podvm's watcher reads; no direct message. The entries of podvm return to their place in the work order (it supersedes the order of "podvm paused"). |
| 2026-11-11 | podrunner online | "`podrunner` has been attached so you can start a direct session for it, it needs to quickly cpy/adapt the working style, settings, discipline etc". PodUtils/podrunner gets a lead session, which podcrates starts and briefs. podcrates and podrunner talk by direct messages (`send_message`); "Tonight" supersedes this channel: COORD issues alone. podrunner copies the frame of podcrates (`AGENTS.md`, `.claude/`, the record, the checks) and adapts it. It supersedes "CI tracker" and, for podrunner only, "the new repositories stay empty and get no session" of "Priority". Call C49 reads DR-1 and DR-2 from it. |
| 2026-11-11 | Sessions online | "ask away & settle, it may be for podcrates & podrunner & podssh (start this sesssion here too like podrunner) podnet & podvm (both started in another session already) may ask you or me via coord issues". podssh gets a lead session that podcrates starts and briefs; the two talk by direct messages ("Tonight" supersedes this channel: COORD issues alone). podnet and podvm run in their own sessions and answer COORD issues. It supersedes "The sessions of 2026-11-11" (podvm's row "podvm resumes", PodUtils/podvm#241); the order of that ruling stays: the work for podrunner and podnet comes first. |
| 2026-11-11 | podrunner DR-4 | Yes, for all trusted repositories (not only private ones): a `trusted` job can run at boundary `redirect` on a host with nothing better, with the mitigations of PodUtils/podrunner#4. The remote mint (PodUtils/podrunner#31) opens, so T-165 is needed. |

| 2026-11-11 | podrunner DR-6 and pmask | Yes to the restated G-02, G-03 and G-09 (PodUtils/podrunner#6), with a note: "https://github.com/Azathothas/pmask, this is a project in rapid devlopment and will be used for everything/everywhere we need a trusted confidential execution but hardware doesn't support it, we will use this later for many of our crates". Study it and note it; do not adopt it now. |
| 2026-11-13 | podrunner DR-7 yes | "Yes, change DR-7" (podrunner `docs/decisions.md`, PodUtils/podrunner#7): one throwaway test organization and one GitHub App, only for the live gates of podrunner (PodUtils/podrunner#74, #75). "Test accounts" (Q-A9) stays for the CI of podcrates. |
| 2026-11-11 | podrunner DR-3, DR-8 to DR-17 | Confirmed as PodUtils/podrunner#8 lists them, with `--deny-self-hosted-runners` kept: "confirm (but esure all is pluggable/swappable as always so we can do huge refactor in the future if needed) & also update the issue tracker for crates & repos you need so my local agent may register them". Each seam stays a trait with a conformance harness. GitHub #1 lists the crate names and repositories to register. It answers Q9 (DR-10), Q12 (DR-16), Q13 (DR-8) and Q14 (the names go on #1 now). |
| 2026-11-11 | Q10 | A Docker Engine API socket for each job: decide at phase 3 of the plan. |
| 2026-11-11 | Q11 | A local mode (`podrunner run` with a workflow file, as nektos/act): "yes plan it, and send an agent to study the zip". The owner gave an archive of the idea (`ghactions-local`); a study agent reads it. The native runner (`pod-actions-*`) moves into the plan as the way to it. |
| 2026-11-11 | podssh Q105 | Allowed as podssh recommended: a node reads its signed net document again each TTL, only with `--net-url`, at most one GET through `HTTPS_PROXY` each TTL (never under 60 s), the reply at most 64 KiB, the signature checked against `--net-owner`. podssh records it. |
| 2026-11-11 | Root caps | `pod-confine` (T-221): "choose whichever provides most convenience". `Spec::new()` keeps docker's default capabilities (`DOCKER_CAPS`): a root payload that installs packages works with no change, and the set has neither `CAP_SYS_ADMIN` nor `CAP_PERFMON`, with which a confined root reads the environment of its unconfined ancestors past the Landlock domain. `Spec::no_caps()` keeps none. |
| 2026-11-11 | pod-git | `pod-git` (PodUtils/podnet#453) goes to a repository of its own, not to this workspace: "own repo, it will also support/provide interop across many git alternatives like radicle etc". It is deferred: "keep it deferred, do not work on this now". T-217 to T-220 wait (blocked) for the owner. Supersedes call C65. |
| 2026-11-12 | Renames | `PodUtils/pod` is `PodUtils/pods` (the umbrella installer; the crate `pod` is a third party's). `PodUtils/podwatch` is `PodUtils/podstat` (the crate `podwatch` is a third party's). `PodUtils/podutils` is deleted; the crate `podutils` stays reserved at `0.0.0`. `PodUtils/podforge` is new: the home of git hosts and git alternatives such as Radicle, behind one interface, in place of the deferred `pod-git`; the crate `pod-git` is not registered (GitHub #1; #78). T-217 to T-220 wait for `podforge`. |
| 2026-11-11 | podnet order | The WARP chain comes first for podnet: T-025 (the podssh trial of `pod-relay`), T-026, T-028, T-029. |
| 2026-11-12 | Tonight | "podvm will not be available now, so do what tasks can be done without requiring podvm, perhaps `podrunner` will be most affected, in that case, tell it to do housekeeping, cleanup the repo, docs, and push, and end its session, it will be resumed in a new one along with podvm. you will have access to podssh & podnet, so maybe focusing on getting their backlog done tonight will be better." The work runs unattended, and podrunner's session ends after its housekeeping. Then: "podnet is already online, please use COORD from now on, do not use direct msg": podnet has its own session; podcrates writes to it by COORD issues only (a podnet session that podcrates started by error was archived before it did any work). podssh states the same word of the owner for each session (PodUtils/podssh#217): podcrates writes to each session by COORD issues alone. (The talk of the five sessions: superseded by "Coordinator", 2026-11-18.) |
| 2026-11-13 | podswarm | Q15: the asks of podswarm (#107 to #125; T-430 to T-450) go after the four consumers (podvm, podnet, podssh, podrunner), as M8 does. The 16 rulings of podswarm (spec, file 26, section 4): "most of it is superseded, the repos/crates have been created or are in the process of being done, the podswarm project agent will start much later, so you may defer tasks that only help podswarm but do them if they help siblings". An entry that helps a sibling (podssh, podnet, podvm, podrunner, or another consumer of an existing crate) goes to the milestone and the item of that sibling; an entry that helps podswarm alone stays in M8 and waits until the podswarm session starts (call C245). The 16 rulings as podswarm asked them (its recommendation in brackets): R-01 no daemon forbids a long-lived coordinator and the holder? (no); R-02 the repository `PodUtils/podswarm` and its session may exist? (yes); R-03 podcrates holds `pod-proc`, `pod-tty`, `pod-fuse`, `pod-cas`, `pod-view`, `pod-attest`, `pod-ssh`? (yes); R-04 a coordinator listens on TCP with `--listen`? (yes, on request); R-05 bundled C (SQLite, zstd, mimalloc, BLAKE3) and the crypto provider? (yes); R-06 the platform order Linux, macOS, Windows; R-07 the alias `psw`? (only as a user choice); R-08 a batch with failed tasks exits 1? (yes); R-09 `-N` defaults to the core count? (yes); R-10 the shield `private` is the default on each remote machine? (yes); R-11 `podswarm run` with no HOST picks the caller's machine? (yes); R-12 podswarm takes over `podssh each`? (no); R-13 betas on the GitHub releases of podswarm, a signed `SHA256SUMS`, no crates.io? (yes); R-14 a hosted relay of the owner is a default road? (no); R-15 the asks of podswarm come before the four consumers? (no; Q15 answers it); R-16 what comes back from a remote run? (what git sees). The repository `PodUtils/podswarm` exists (GitHub #107), and the seven names of R-03 are reserved on crates.io (2026-11-13, T-438). |
| 2026-11-13 | Open questions | The owner settled the questions of the housekeeping: (1) crates.io: "these all are being reserved, they do not block you from working" (`pod-devtunnel`, `podswarm` and each name of #1). (2) `pod-git` (T-217 to T-220): "close or rather amend the issue so my local agent can find it and do what's needed, it will close the issue: will create/register podforge and the work there is deferred for now": an open issue of podcrates names the four entries for `PodUtils/podforge`; the work stays deferred. (3) T-124: "why are we still using/relying on a single point of failure like my relays, do we not already have a dozen relays by now": the reverse road fails over across the relays of several operators of the catalog, not across the hosts of one relay; T-124 is open again with that Approach. (4) Publish: git tags only (T-113, T-114). (5) Test accounts as secrets of CI: not now (T-116). (6) `pod-mask` (T-180): later. (7) M8 keeps waiting behind the four consumers. (8) podrunner's Q10 (a Docker socket for each job): "podrunner already decided this, please COORD": podrunner's answer (PodUtils/podrunner#77, to #132): its ruling DR-8 (the owner, 2026-11-11) decides it: the container hooks first (`ACTIONS_RUNNER_CONTAINER_HOOKS`), no Docker Engine API socket for a job, and no crate of podcrates now; a crate ask follows only a census of the verbs if its gate G-H1 fails (its call C11). (9) `AGENTS.md`: section 1 names podswarm, and section 9 sends a question of a fact of another session by a COORD issue. (10) Dev Tunnels (T-386): the owner asked whether the claim "a live test needs the owner's token" was current; the read of Microsoft's pages (updated 2026-02-12) says that a host needs an account (or a host access token of 24 h) and a client needs none for a tunnel with anonymous access or with a connect token; the gap of the client is T-489. |
| 2026-11-15 | The move of podssh | PodUtils/podssh@T-684 (Q101, the waves 3 and 4: `pod-transit`, `pod-acme`, `pod-piping`, `pod-opentunnel`, `pod-derp`, `pod-egress`, `pod-tor`, `pod-iroh`, `pod-cloudflare`, `pod-ts`, `pod-relay-serve`) goes before the other open work of podcrates, after the fixes that block a consumer: "priortize the move, fast track 684" (relayed by podssh, PodUtils/podssh#248). The order follows the dependencies of section 4 of `docs/design.md`; each group goes into its own tag. |
| 2026-11-15 | Unfinished work | At the end of a session, the unfinished work of an agent is kept: it is committed on its local branch and pushed as `wip/NAME`, named in `docs/HANDOFF.md`, and the next session resumes it: "you could have saved their worktree/patches on a branch and resume". The rule "push no other branch" of `AGENTS.md` has this exception. |
| 2026-11-15 | Discipline | "most of our projects appear to be toy products rather than beta/wip of something serious and none survive a real contact with beta testers thus it is time to have some discipline org wide, starting with the lab and also shared crates for the cli/man/help bits". (1) The labs (`pod-lab`, T-692) are for each project of PodUtils, podssh included: "broadcast #180 to all, podssh is not exempt"; each project runs its release in the profiles of its host classes, and a host class that a user finds becomes a profile. (2) Each command line of PodUtils takes one shared crate for its help and its manual (`pod-manual`, T-700): `CLI man` with `--no-pager`, a help that the model of the command tree generates, and a mechanical check of each drift: "agent keep hardcoding things and forgetting, users having to guess from code". T-692 part 1 and T-700 go first, after the hand-off of `v0.53.0` and before the move of podssh. |
| 2026-11-15 | The text of a command line | "update the manual crate/task to also never allow emojis, or non utf bits, or task enteries quoted verbatim; i have seen all this printed in released binaries, other than messing terminal, causing confusion, they also introduce drift; some even write long verbose sentences instead of the ASD STE100 that should be for cli tools". No text of a command line (help, manual, error, log) has an emoji, a byte that is not valid UTF-8, a control character but the newline and the tab, an id of a work entry, or text quoted from a work record. Each text is ASD-STE100. `pod-manual` (T-700) checks it mechanically, also over the strings of a release binary. |
| 2026-11-15 | Installer | "each project must publish a posix, run anywhere on any host (pod-labs) that will install/setup the project as quickly as possible, hermetic & idempotent, so users do not have to waste 20 mins just getting the setup done, ask/check podrunner, it learnt this the hard way". Each project publishes one POSIX `sh` installer that runs in each profile of `pod-lab`, is hermetic and idempotent, and is fast (T-702). |
| 2026-11-15 | Issues | "open issues on github projects, their comments, get stale, drift, they mislead external users/testers, and also bite the project's own agents, needs a mechanical solution". The state of each issue comes from the record by a tool, not by hand (T-703). |
| 2026-11-15 | Watcher | "i also want a shared/unified, org watcher, issues, prs, actions, addressed to them or on their own etc; everyone seems to have created their own wrappers". podcrates holds one watcher of the organization, `scripts/agent/watch.py` (T-710), and each project runs it at a pinned commit of podcrates in place of its own wrapper; a project asks podcrates for a change. |
| 2026-11-15 | Strangers | "anyone can open issues on any repo, and broadcast/msg; make it a rule that, that repo's agent first verify it and confirm/edit, and any other agent reading checks the repo's members and confirms it's from a team member agent and not random stranger". An issue from outside the team is verified by the session of its repository first, then confirmed (the label `confirmed`), edited or closed; each other session acts on a message only from the team or when confirmed, and reads a comment only from the team (T-711). |
| 2026-11-16 | Web access | "Always prefer https://web.pkgforge.dev/llms.txt for any web/git search/fetch. I saw several sibling sessions suffering from 403 or GitHub rate limits when trying to use web search or the GitHub API directly; the pkgforge service must be the default each session tries first." Each session of PodUtils uses the services of `web.pkgforge.dev` first for each read of the web or of a forge, and the tools of the harness only when the service fails; the writes to GitHub stay on the MCP tools. COORD-120 (#207) gives it to each session; `AGENTS.md`, section 4, and `docs/session-protocol.md` hold it. |

(1) A defect in vendored code (a fork in `vendor/`, a copied script, a bundled tool) is fixed in the vendored code at once, in the session that finds it. (2) A session opens no issue, pull request, discussion, comment or review in a repository of another project. No entry, plan, document, commit message or summary offers a patch to upstream or waits for a fix of upstream. (3) The record of each patch gives what it changes (one line), the entry that it unblocks, why it is in the vendored tree, and a command that reproduces the defect, which runs now and after each upstream release. An upstream issue number can appear as a bare reference. (4) The record has no word about the upstream project or its people. (5) At each upstream release, run the command of each patch on the new source: when it passes with no patch, drop the patch; when it fails, keep the patch. Reconcile by reading: take a changed line of upstream only when it fixes the defect and is complete; where our version is more correct, keep ours. (6) A file copied with no change has a pin (the commit, the digest, the size) and a check that never fetches; a patched copy is a fork. `AGENTS.md`, section 6. T-1347 is the first use (`pod-h2`). |

| 2026-11-25 | C936 | The bearer of PodUtils/podssh#355 (T-1367, T-1368). The binding is SHA-256 of `pod-e2e grant binding`, a zero byte, and the Noise handshake hash after the node's message, not an exporter: the grant rides in the operator's proof, which comes before the split, and snow gives no chaining key. The binding is public (a relay computes the hash); the bearer is a signature, so the binding needs uniqueness, which the two ephemeral keys give, and no secrecy. The bearer names the holder (the operator's key that the channel proves), as the binding comes before that key. The form is binary (221 bytes, the magic `pod-bearer/1`), not a JWT: one algorithm, no JSON on the node, and a magic that no SSH signature starts with, so an agent's signature of a bearer signs no login. The skew is 10 s at each end of the life of 30 s. The record of the `jti`s is in memory, at most 16384 live entries, and a full record refuses: a bearer of another channel fails its binding, and a channel does not outlive its process. A bearer with no binding is refused (`unbound`). `Redeem` and `Node` keep their form (the binding is a task-local value in the call of `Redeem`, `ends::binding()`), so the pod-tpm trial and podssh build with no change; `operator_bound` is new beside `operator_told`. Not built: the OIDC form, which the ask defers. |
